It’s gotten to the point where it feels completely out of my hands, and I really need some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of overeating happen mostly at night after your shift, and do you also find that you eat very little or have little appetite during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen mostly at night after my cleaning shifts or when I'm up late. As for the daytime, I'm not really sure and haven't noticed if my appetite is low or if I'm eating less then.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever try to make up for eating by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising a lot? Also, do you feel upset or guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: I feel incredibly guilty, ashamed, and disgusted with myself afterward, but I don't ever try to throw up, fast, or use laxatives to make up for it. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?"

Also, do you feel upset or guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: I feel incredibly guilty, ashamed, and disgusted with myself afterward, but I don't ever try to throw up, fast, or use laxatives to make up for it. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. When I'm feeling stressed, bored, or frustrated with my art, I'll end up eating like that even if I'm not actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone at least 2 hours without eating during the day or after waking up, then had a strong urge to eat late at night or after midnight that feels much bigger than a normal meal?\n\nPatient: I definitely get really strong urges to eat huge amounts of food late at night or after my shifts. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?"
  }
]

No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.

supervisor’s comment about \"raiding the breakroom\" left her embarrassed enough to bring it up at a campus counseling center referral.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I can’t stop or control how much I’m eating.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S049 (Eating_Large_Amount): On some nights after cleaning, I’ll stand in the breakroom or my because I’m so ashamed of myself.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_frequency: For at least the last several months, there’s been at least one night every single week—often more—where I breakroom leftovers.\n- duration: This pattern of out-of-control late-night eating has been going on pretty steadily since I picked up extra night shifts and cut back on classes about a year and a Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. It’s gotten to the point where it feels completely out of my hands, and I really need some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of overeating happen mostly at night after your shift, and do you also find that you eat very little or have little appetite during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen mostly at night after my cleaning shifts or when I'm up late. As for the daytime, I'm not really sure and haven't noticed if my appetite is low or if I'm eating less then.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever try to make up for eating by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising a lot? Also, do you feel upset or guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: I feel incredibly guilty, ashamed, and disgusted with myself afterward, but I don't ever try to throw up, fast, or use laxatives to make up for it. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. When I'm feeling stressed, bored, or frustrated with my art, I'll end up eating like that even if I'm not actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone at least 2 hours without eating during the day or after waking up, then had a strong urge to eat late at night or after midnight that feels much bigger than a normal meal?\n\nPatient: I definitely get really strong urges to eat huge amounts of food late at night or after my shifts. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It started pretty steadily after I took on more night shifts and cut back on my classes over a year ago. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. Even though it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I lose control, I am fully aware of what I'm doing and even think to myself \"you need to stop\" while eating. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?"

grab from my cupboards in under an hour.\n- S050 (Loss_of_Control): Once I start, it feels like something in my brain flips off and I’m just shoveling food in even while I’m thinking, ‘you need to stop, this is too much,’ but I literally can’t make myself put it down until everything in front of me is gone.\n- S051 (Rapid_Eating): I eat so fast during these episodes that I barely chew or taste anything—just swallowing one bite after another like I’m racing a clock, and I only realize how quickly I went when I look down and all the wrappers are already piled up.\n- S053 (Eating_Without_Hunger): A lot of the time I start eating right after a normal meal or when I’m just bored and stressed about my unfinished design projects, and even though I don’t feel hungry at all, I’ll still go hunting through vending machines or the fridge and keep eating past feeling full and uncomfortable.\n- S055 (Negative_Affect_After_Binge): When I finally stop, I just sit there staring at the empty containers, feeling disgusting and weak and thinking, ‘you did it again, no wonder you’re gaining weight and not getting your life together,’ and it can ruin the rest of my day or morning because I’m so ashamed of myself.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_frequency: For at least the last several months, there’s been at least one night every single week—often more—where I have one of these out-of-control eating episodes after my shift or when I’m up late avoiding my school and design work.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping morning classes and turning down basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed after these nights, and even at work I’m more distracted and anxious about coworkers noticing how much I eat from the breakroom leftovers.\n- duration: This pattern of out-of-control late-night eating has been going on pretty steadily since I picked up extra night shifts and cut back on classes about a year and a couple of months ago.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. I’ll still go hunting through vending machines or the fridge and keep eating past feeling full and uncomfortable.\n- S055 (Negative_Affect_After_Binge): When I finally stop, I just sit there staring at the empty containers, feeling disgusting and weak and thinking, ‘you did it again, no wonder you’re gaining weight and not getting your life together,’ and it can ruin the rest of my day or morning because I’m so ashamed of myself.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_frequency: For at least the last several months, there’s been at least one night every single week—often more—where I have one of these out-of-control eating episodes after my shift or when I’m up late avoiding my school and design work.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping morning classes and turning down basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed after these nights, and even at work I’m more distracted and anxious about coworkers noticing how much I eat from the breakroom leftovers.\n- duration: This pattern of out-of-control late-night eating has been going on pretty steadily since I picked up extra night shifts and cut back on classes about a year and a couple of months ago.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I can’t stop or control how much I’m eating.\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. It’s gotten to the point where it feels completely out of my hands, and I really need some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of overeating happen mostly at night after your shift, and do you also find that you eat very little or have little appetite during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen mostly at night after my cleaning shifts or when I'm up late. As for the daytime, I'm not really sure and haven't noticed if my appetite is low or if I'm eating less then.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever try to make up for eating by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising a lot? Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes? Also, has your weight or body shape had a major effect on how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't throw up, fast, or exercise to make up for it—I've actually stopped playing basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed. My weight gain has a massive, negative impact on how I see myself, making me feel disgusting, weak, and like I just can't get my life together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of trying to lose weight by strict dieting, fasting, or other behaviors to compensate for these binges, or is the main issue the binge episodes themselves?"

grab from my cupboards in under an hour.\n- S050 (Loss_of_Control): Once I start, it feels like something in my brain flips off and I’m just shoveling food in even while I’m thinking, ‘you need to stop, this is too much,’ but I literally can’t make myself put it down until everything in front of me is gone.\n- S051 (Rapid_Eating): I eat so fast during these episodes that I barely chew or taste anything—just swallowing one bite after another like I’m racing a clock, and I only realize how quickly I went when I look down and all the wrappers are already piled up.\n- S053 (Eating_Without_Hunger): A lot of the time I start eating right after a normal meal or when I’m just bored and stressed about my unfinished design projects, and even though I don’t feel hungry at all, I’ll still go hunting through vending machines or the fridge and keep eating past feeling full and uncomfortable.\n- S055 (Negative_Affect_After_Binge): When I finally stop, I just sit there staring at the empty containers, feeling disgusting and weak and thinking, ‘you did it again, no wonder you’re gaining weight and not getting your life together,’ and it can ruin the rest of my day or morning because I’m so ashamed of myself.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_frequency: For at least the last several months, there’s been at least one night every single week—often more—where I have one of these out-of-control eating episodes after my shift or when I’m up late avoiding my school and design work.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping morning classes and turning down basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed after these nights, and even at work I’m more distracted and anxious about coworkers noticing how much I eat from the breakroom leftovers.\n- duration: This pattern of out-of-control late-night eating has been going on pretty steadily since I picked up extra night shifts and cut back on classes about a year and a couple of months ago.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I can’t stop or control how much I’m eating.\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: binge_frequency, duration, S049 (Eating_Large_Amount), S050 (Loss_of_Control)\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that this has been happening at least once a week (often more) for much longer than three months—actually for over a year. Explain that these episodes definitely involve eating a massive, clearly large amount of food (like eating half a catering tray, multiple sandwiches, pizza, and cookies in under an hour) and describe the intense feeling of losing control, where you want to stop but your brain feels like it flips off and you can't put the food down."

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It started pretty steadily after I took on more night shifts and cut back on my classes over a year ago. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes? Also, has your weight or body shape had a major effect on how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't throw up, fast, or exercise to make up for it—I've actually stopped playing basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed. My weight gain has a massive, negative impact on how I see myself, making me feel disgusting, weak, and like I just can't get my life together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of trying to lose weight by strict dieting, fasting, or other behaviors to compensate for these binges, or is the main issue the binge episodes themselves?\n\nPatient: The main issue is definitely just the out-of-control binge episodes themselves, as I don't do any strict dieting, fasting, or purging to compensate. Instead, I've ended up gaining weight and have even stopped running and playing basketball with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge episodes happen at least once a week for the past 3 months, and do they involve eating a clearly large amount of food with a loss of control?"

at the empty containers, feeling disgusting and weak and thinking, ‘you did it again, no wonder you’re gaining weight and not getting your life together,’ and it can ruin the rest of my day or morning because I’m so ashamed of myself.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_frequency: For at least the last several months, there’s been at least one night every single week—often more—where I have one of these out-of-control eating episodes after my shift or when I’m up late avoiding my school and design work.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping morning classes and turning down basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed after these nights, and even at work I’m more distracted and anxious about coworkers noticing how much I eat from the Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. It’s gotten to the point where it feels completely out of my hands, and I really need some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of overeating happen mostly at night after your shift, and do you also find that you eat very little or have little appetite during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen mostly at night after my cleaning shifts or when I'm up late. As for the daytime, I'm not really sure and haven't noticed if my appetite is low or if I'm eating less then.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever try to make up for eating by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising a lot? Also, do you feel upset or guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: I feel incredibly guilty, ashamed, and disgusted with myself afterward, but I don't ever try to throw up, fast, or use laxatives to make up for it. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It started pretty steadily after I took on more night shifts and cut back on my classes over a year ago. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. Even though it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I lose control, I am fully aware of what I'm doing and even think to myself \"you need to stop\" while eating. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes? My weight gain has a massive, negative impact on how I see myself, making me feel disgusting, weak, and like I just can't get my life together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of trying to lose weight by strict dieting, fasting, or other behaviors to compensate for these binges, or is the main issue the binge episodes themselves?\n\nPatient: The main issue is definitely just the out-of-control binge episodes themselves, as I don't do any strict dieting, fasting, or purging to compensate. Instead, I've ended up gaining weight and have even stopped running and playing basketball with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge episodes happen at least once a week for the past 3 months, and do they involve eating a clearly large amount of food with a loss of control?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been happening at least once a week—often more—for over a year now, which is much longer than three months. They definitely involve eating a massive, clearly large amount of food, like polishing off half a catering tray, multiple sandwiches, and cookies in under an hour, while feeling a total loss of control where I want to stop but my brain just flips off and I can't put the food down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you eat a very large amount of food in a short period but do not feel a loss of control, or is every episode a true binge where you feel unable to stop?"

As her art projects stalled and she felt further from her dream of becoming a designer, she became more reliant on solitary eating at odd hours to cope with frustration and the guilt of procrastinated coursework and paperwork. She finally sought psychiatric care when her eating patterns began to feel out of control, she noticed significant weight gain affecting her comfort playing basketball and running with the club, and a supervisor’s comment about \"raiding the breakroom\" left her embarrassed enough to bring it up at a campus counseling center referral.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I can’t stop or control how much I’m eating.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S049 (Eating_Large_Amount): On some nights after cleaning, I’ll stand in the breakroom or my tiny kitchen and just keep going—leftover pizza, a couple of sandwiches, chips, cookies—way more than anyone else on my crew would eat, until I’ve gone through half a catering tray and whatever I can grab from my cupboards in under an hour.\n- S050 (Loss_of_Control): Once I start, it feels like something in my brain flips off and I’m just shoveling food in even while I’m thinking, ‘you need to at the empty containers, feeling disgusting and weak and thinking, ‘you did it again, no wonder you’re gaining weight and not getting your life together,’ and it can ruin the rest of my day or morning because I’m so ashamed of myself.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_frequency: For at least the last several months, there’s been at least one night every single week—often more—where I have one of these out-of-control eating episodes after my shift or when I’m up late avoiding my school and design work.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping morning classes and turning down basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed after these nights, and even at work I’m more distracted and anxious about coworkers noticing how much I eat from the breakroom leftovers.\n- duration: This pattern of out-of-control late-night eating has been going on pretty steadily since I picked up extra night shifts and cut back on classes about a year and a couple of months ago.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. It’s gotten to the point where it feels completely out of my hands, and I really need some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of overeating happen mostly at night after your shift, and do you also find that you eat very little or have little appetite during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen mostly at night after my cleaning shifts or when I'm up late. As for the daytime, I'm not really sure and haven't noticed if my appetite is low or if I'm eating less then.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever try to make up for eating by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising a lot? Also, do you feel upset or guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: I feel incredibly guilty, ashamed, and disgusted with myself afterward, but I don't ever try to throw up, fast, or use laxatives to make up for it. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. When I'm feeling stressed, bored, or frustrated with my art, I'll end up eating like that even if I'm not actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone at least 2 hours without eating during the day or after waking up, then had a strong urge to eat late at night or after midnight that feels much bigger than a normal meal?\n\nPatient: I definitely get really strong urges to eat huge amounts of food late at night or after my shifts. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It started pretty steadily after I took on more night shifts and cut back on my classes over a year ago. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. Even though it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I lose control, I am fully aware of what I'm doing and even think to myself \"you need to stop\" while eating. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes? Also, has your weight or body shape had a major effect on how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't throw up, fast, or exercise to make up for it—I've actually stopped playing basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed. My weight gain has a massive, negative impact on how I see myself, making me feel disgusting, weak, and like I just can't get my life together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of trying to lose weight by strict dieting, fasting, or other behaviors to compensate for these binges, or is the main issue the binge episodes themselves?\n\nPatient: The main issue is definitely just the out-of-control binge episodes themselves, as I don't do any strict dieting, fasting, or purging to compensate. Instead, I've ended up gaining weight and have even stopped running and playing basketball with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge episodes happen at least once a week for the past 3 months, and do they involve eating a clearly large amount of food with a loss of control?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been happening at least once a week—often more—for over a year now, which is much longer than three months. They definitely involve eating a massive, clearly large amount of food, like polishing off half a catering tray, multiple sandwiches, and cookies in under an hour, while feeling a total loss of control where I want to stop but my brain just flips off and I can't put the food down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you eat a very large amount of food in a short period but do not feel a loss of control, or is every episode a true binge where you feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: Every single one of these episodes involves a complete loss of control. Once I start eating those large amounts of food, it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I literally cannot stop myself or put the food down until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Do your binge episodes happen mainly during a specific period of the day or night, or do they occur at any time when you feel stressed or out of control?"

Even though stress and boredom are the main triggers, the timing is pretty much always during those late-night hours.

Instead, I've ended up gaining weight and have even stopped running and playing basketball with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge episodes happen at least once a week for the past 3 months, and do they involve eating a clearly large amount of food with a loss of control?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been happening at least once a week—often more—for over a year now, which is much longer than three months. They definitely involve eating a massive, clearly large amount of food, like polishing off half a catering tray, multiple sandwiches, and cookies in under an hour, while feeling a total loss of control where I want to stop but my brain just flips off and I can't put the food down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you eat a very large amount of food in a short period but do not feel a loss of control, or is every episode a true binge where you feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: Every single one of these episodes involves a complete loss of control. Once I start eating those large amounts of food, it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I literally cannot stop myself or put the food down until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Do your binge episodes happen mainly during a specific period of the day or night, or do they occur at any time when you feel stressed or out of control?\n\nPatient: They almost always happen late at night or in the early morning hours, especially right after my night shifts as a cleaner or when I'm staying up late avoiding my school and design work. Even though stress and boredom are the main triggers, the timing is pretty much always during those late-night hours."
  }

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. When I'm feeling stressed, bored, or frustrated with my art, I'll end up eating like that even if I'm not actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone at least 2 hours without eating during the day or after waking up, then had a strong urge to eat late at night or after midnight that feels much bigger than a normal meal?\n\nPatient: I definitely get really strong urges to eat huge amounts of food late at night or after my shifts. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. Even though it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I lose control, I am fully aware of what I'm doing and even think to myself \"you need to stop\" while eating. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes?     "content": "Candidate Diseases: \n[\"F50.8\", \"F50.9\"]\n\nInterview Transcript: \nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really struggling because I feel like once I start eating after my night shifts, I just can’t stop or control how much I’m eating. It’s gotten to the point where it feels completely out of my hands, and I really need some help with it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of overeating happen mostly at night after your shift, and do you also find that you eat very little or have little appetite during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen mostly at night after my cleaning shifts or when I'm up late. As for the daytime, I'm not really sure and haven't noticed if my appetite is low or if I'm eating less then.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes, do you ever try to make up for eating by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or exercising a lot? Also, do you feel upset or guilty afterward?\n\nPatient: I feel incredibly guilty, ashamed, and disgusted with myself afterward, but I don't ever try to throw up, fast, or use laxatives to make up for it. Instead of over-exercising, I actually end up skipping my usual basketball and running club sessions because I feel so sluggish and embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: When you are not working night shifts, do you still have episodes of eating a very large amount of food with a sense of losing control, or does this mostly happen only after being awake late at night?\n\nPatient: It actually doesn't just happen on nights I work. I also have these out-of-control episodes on my nights off, especially when I'm staying up late at home trying to avoid my schoolwork or design projects. When I'm feeling stressed, bored, or frustrated with my art, I'll end up eating like that even if I'm not actually hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gone at least 2 hours without eating during the day or after waking up, then had a strong urge to eat late at night or after midnight that feels much bigger than a normal meal?\n\nPatient: I definitely get really strong urges to eat huge amounts of food late at night or after my shifts. However, I'm not sure about the pattern of going two hours without eating during the day, since my eating schedule is so irregular and I often eat even when I'm not hungry.\n\nDoctor: When these late-night eating episodes happen, do you usually wake up from sleep to eat, or are they happening while you are still awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: No, I don't wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes always happen while I'm still awake, either right after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my design and schoolwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat most of your calories after dinner or late at night, but without feeling like you’re having distinct binge episodes with a loss of control and eating unusually large amounts?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not just normal late-night eating. These are distinct episodes where I feel a complete loss of control and eat massive, unusual amounts of food—like sandwiches, chips, and leftovers—all in under an hour. Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It started pretty steadily after I took on more night shifts and cut back on my classes over a year ago. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. Even though it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I lose control, I am fully aware of what I'm doing and even think to myself \"you need to stop\" while eating. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes? Also, has your weight or body shape had a major effect on how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't throw up, fast, or exercise to make up for it—I've actually stopped playing basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed. My weight gain has a massive, negative impact on how I see myself, making me feel disgusting, weak, and like I just can't get my life together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of trying to lose weight by strict dieting, fasting, or other behaviors to compensate for these binges, or is the main issue the binge episodes themselves?\n\nPatient: The main issue is definitely just the out-of-control binge episodes themselves, as I don't do any strict dieting, fasting, or purging to compensate. Instead, I've ended up gaining weight and have even stopped running and playing basketball with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge episodes happen at least once a week for the past 3 months, and do they involve eating a clearly large amount of food with a loss of control?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been happening at least once a week—often more—for over a year now, which is much longer than three months. They definitely involve eating a massive, clearly large amount of food, like polishing off half a catering tray, multiple sandwiches, and cookies in under an hour, while feeling a total loss of control where I want to stop but my brain just flips off and I can't put the food down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you eat a very large amount of food in a short period but do not feel a loss of control, or is every episode a true binge where you feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: Every single one of these episodes involves a complete loss of control. Once I start eating those large amounts of food, it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I literally cannot stop myself or put the food down until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Do your binge episodes happen mainly during a specific period of the day or night, or do they occur at any time when you feel stressed or out of control?\n\nPatient: They almost always happen late at night or in the early morning hours, especially right after my night shifts as a cleaner or when I'm staying up late avoiding my school and design work. Even though stress and boredom are the main triggers, the timing is pretty much always during those late-night hours.\n\nDoctor: Do your binge episodes happen almost exclusively at night or after you’ve gone to sleep and then woken up to eat, or do they also happen during the daytime when you are fully awake?\n\nPatient: They only happen when I am fully awake, and I never wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes typically happen late at night after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my school and design work, usually when I'm stressed and trying to unwind."

Once I start, it feels like I literally can't make myself stop until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these binge-eating episodes regularly for at least 3 months, with at least one episode per week on average?\n\nPatient: Yes, this has been happening at least once a week—often more—for about fourteen months now. It started pretty steadily after I took on more night shifts and cut back on my classes over a year ago. It's honestly really exhausting and embarrassing that it's been going on for this long.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up from sleep specifically to eat large amounts of food, or do these episodes only happen while you are fully awake and staying up late?\n\nPatient: They only happen while I'm fully awake. I don't ever wake up from sleep to eat; it's always when I'm already up late after my cleaning shifts or when I'm avoiding my school and design work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat large amounts of food automatically or with little awareness while half-asleep, or is it always a fully conscious binge where you clearly remember starting and stopping?\n\nPatient: I am always fully awake and conscious during these episodes, never half-asleep. Even though it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I lose control, I am fully aware of what I'm doing and even think to myself \"you need to stop\" while eating. I remember every bit of it afterward, which just leaves me feeling incredibly ashamed and disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most of your food intake happens after dinner or late at night without distinct binge episodes, or are these clearly separate binge episodes where you eat a very large amount in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: These are definitely distinct, out-of-control binge episodes rather than just general late-night snacking. Once I start, I eat a massive amount of food like leftover catering, sandwiches, and snacks very quickly, usually in under an hour. It really feels like a switch in my brain has flipped off, and I'm completely unable to stop even when I want to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to control your weight by dieting, fasting, vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively after these episodes? Also, has your weight or body shape had a major effect on how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don't throw up, fast, or exercise to make up for it—I've actually stopped playing basketball and running with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed. My weight gain has a massive, negative impact on how I see myself, making me feel disgusting, weak, and like I just can't get my life together.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of trying to lose weight by strict dieting, fasting, or other behaviors to compensate for these binges, or is the main issue the binge episodes themselves?\n\nPatient: The main issue is definitely just the out-of-control binge episodes themselves, as I don't do any strict dieting, fasting, or purging to compensate. Instead, I've ended up gaining weight and have even stopped running and playing basketball with my club because I feel too sluggish and ashamed.\n\nDoctor: Do these binge episodes happen at least once a week for the past 3 months, and do they involve eating a clearly large amount of food with a loss of control?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been happening at least once a week—often more—for over a year now, which is much longer than three months. They definitely involve eating a massive, clearly large amount of food, like polishing off half a catering tray, multiple sandwiches, and cookies in under an hour, while feeling a total loss of control where I want to stop but my brain just flips off and I can't put the food down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you eat a very large amount of food in a short period but do not feel a loss of control, or is every episode a true binge where you feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: Every single one of these episodes involves a complete loss of control. Once I start eating those large amounts of food, it feels like a switch flips in my brain and I literally cannot stop myself or put the food down until everything in front of me is gone.\n\nDoctor: Do your binge episodes happen mainly during a specific period of the day or night, or do they occur at any time when you feel stressed or out of control?\n\nPatient: They almost always happen late at night or in the early morning hours, especially right after my night shifts as a cleaner or when I'm staying up late avoiding my school and design work. Even though stress and boredom are the main triggers, the timing is pretty much always during those late-night hours.\n\nDoctor: Do your binge episodes happen almost exclusively at night or after you’ve gone to sleep and then woken up to eat, or do they also happen during the daytime when you are fully awake?\n\nPatient: They only happen when I am fully awake, and I never wake up from sleep to eat. These episodes typically happen late at night after my night shifts or when I'm staying up late avoiding my school and design work, usually when I'm stressed and trying to unwind.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen late at night, do you feel fully awake and aware the whole time, or do you sometimes eat in a half-asleep or automatic state and only remember it vaguely afterward?\n\nPatient: I am completely awake and aware during these episodes, and I actually often start eating to help stay awake during my late shifts or while studying. I am fully conscious of what I'm doing and even think to myself that I need to stop while it's happening, so I remember everything clearly, which is why I feel so much regret and shame afterward."

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.
